\chapter{Overview}

\section{What this project is}

This blueprint tracks the machine-checked formalization, in Lean~4 with Mathlib, of the
\emph{shadow framework} developed by Daniel Toupin. Every statement marked
\leanok\ below has been checked by Lean's kernel. Everything else is stated
honestly as open.

\section{What it is trying to find out}

The organising question is not a single conjecture. It is a correspondence:

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Analytic number theory and celestial holography appear to be describing the same
object from two sides. What is that object, and how far does the dictionary go?}
\end{quote}

On the number-theory side sit the completed zeta function $\xi(s)$, its functional
equation $s \mapsto 1-s$, the id\`ele class group $\mathbb{A}^\times/\mathbb{Q}^\times$
with its Haar measure, and the Weil explicit formula. On the holography side sit
celestial amplitudes, the principal series, Mellin transforms, and the shadow transform
$\Delta \mapsto 2-\Delta$ on conformal dimensions.

The shared structure is a \textbf{shadow involution}. Under the dictionary
$\Delta = 2s$ it is the same map on both sides, and its fixed locus is exactly the
critical line --- proved here as
\texttt{GppFE.critical\_line\_is\_fixed\_locus}: for $s \in \mathbb{C}$,
$\overline{s} = 1-s \iff \operatorname{Re}(s) = \tfrac12$. (The involution that matters
is $s \mapsto 1-\overline{s}$, not $s \mapsto 1-s$; the latter fixes only the single
point $s = \tfrac12$.)

Because the involution is shared, so are its consequences. That is what makes the
correspondence worth formalizing rather than merely asserting: each transfer either
survives kernel-checking or fails in a specific, nameable place.

\section{Shadow holography}

The framework reads the shared involution holographically, at three nested levels, each a
Mellin or Pontryagin duality and therefore an exact dictionary rather than an analogy.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Line and sphere.} The multiplicative line $\mathbb{R}_+$, split at its fixed
    point $x=1$, is the boundary. The spectral sphere, split by the critical line (the
    \emph{equator}) into two hemispheres, is its hologram. Inversion $x\mapsto 1/x$ becomes
    the reflection $s\mapsto 1-\overline{s}$, and support on a half-line becomes analyticity
    on a hemisphere.
  \item \textbf{Integers as modes.} The integers are the Fourier modes of a circle, and
    Poisson self-duality of that lattice becomes the functional equation.
  \item \textbf{Primes as a torus.} By unique factorization the primes span a compact torus
    $\prod_p S^1$ dual to $\mathbb{Q}_+^\times$, and the critical line embeds in it densely:
    a hologram inside a hologram.
\end{enumerate}
On the celestial side the same equator is the scale sector, since boosts act on energy by
dilation. This is an exact structural parallel. It is not a claim that $\xi$ is a
correlator of a physical theory.

In this language the functional equation says the hologram is \emph{symmetric}; the Riemann
Hypothesis says it is also \emph{positive} (reflection-positive across the equator). The
finite core of that statement is proved
(\texttt{GppHolographicRP.reflectionForm\_nonneg\_iff}: positivity across the equator
holds exactly when there are no off-equator mirror pairs). Constructing that positivity for
the actual arithmetic distribution is equivalent to RH and remains open.

\section{Goals, in order of ambition}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Map the dictionary precisely.} For each object on one side, identify its
    counterpart on the other, and prove the transfer or name exactly what blocks it.
  \item \textbf{Understand the shadow involution as a single mathematical object} rather
    than as two coincidentally similar symmetries.
  \item \textbf{The Riemann Hypothesis} as one open question inside the framework: the
    positivity half of the hologram. Several routes to it have been developed here and
    several recorded as dead ends; none is the thesis of the project, and the tower has
    value whether or not any of them closes.
  \item \textbf{Physical consequences} of the same involution: CPT and time reversal,
    complete positivity, three fermion generations, the Einstein equations as a rigidity
    statement.
\end{enumerate}

\section{How to read this blueprint}

Results carry \leanok\ when the Lean proof is complete and kernel-checked. A statement
without it is open, and the surrounding text says what is missing.

Three categories are tracked separately and never conflated:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \texttt{sorry} --- \textbf{zero}, and CI fails the build if any appears.
  \item custom \texttt{axiom} --- \textbf{zero}, down from thirteen. The last one,
    \texttt{exp\_growth\_not\_tempered}, was proved on 2026-08-31 once the move to
    Mathlib 4.33 made a concrete nonzero Schwartz witness constructible.
  \item \texttt{True}-stubs --- open results deliberately parked. Every one is named
    \texttt{open\_}\ldots, and CI rejects any that is not. A file full of stubs still
    reports ``0 sorry, 0 axiom'', so the prefix is what keeps that honest.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{What is deliberately not claimed.} Nobody proves RH in this repository by
accident. The value is that every reduction and constant in the surrounding tower is
kernel-checked, with each remaining gap named precisely. Where a route was tried and
failed, the failure is recorded rather than quietly dropped.

\section{Coverage caveat}

This blueprint is hand-authored; it does not regenerate itself from the Lean source.
Proved material can therefore exist in the repository before it appears in the chapters
below. Absence from this document is not evidence that something is unproved --- check
the Lean source.

\chapter{Haar Measure Foundation}

\section{Self-Duality on Compact Groups}

\begin{lemma}[Haar Measure Preserved by Automorphisms]
  \label{lem:haar-invariant}
  \lean{haar_invariant_under_automorphism}
  \leanok
  Let $G$ be a compact second-countable topological group, $\mu$ a Haar measure,
  and $\phi: G \to G$ a bicontinuous group automorphism. Then
  $\phi_*\mu = \mu$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  Proved clean in \texttt{HaarSelfDuality.lean} (zero sorries, zero axioms).
  Uses \texttt{MulEquiv.isHaarMeasure\_map} and mass preservation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Grassmannian Haar Self-Duality]
  \label{thm:haar-self-dual}
  \lean{grassmannian_haar_self_duality}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:haar-invariant}
  The shadow involution $\phi: g \mapsto g^{-1}$ preserves the Haar measure
  on any compact group (instance: $\mathrm{Gr}(2,4)$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Ad\`elic Haar Self-Duality]
  \label{thm:adelic-haar}
  \lean{GppHaar.adelic_haar_self_dual}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:haar-invariant}
  On any compact topological group, $\phi_*\mu = \mu$ for $\phi = (-)^{-1}$.
  Instance: $\mathbb{A}^\times/\mathbb{Q}^\times$ with Haar measure $d^\times a$
  satisfies $d^\times(a^{-1}) = d^\times a$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[Compact Factor $K^1 = \mathbb{A}^1/\mathbb{Q}^\times$]
  \label{lem:fujisaki}
  \lean{GppHaar.open_adelic_quotient_compact_factor}
  \uses{thm:adelic-haar}
  The norm-$1$ id\`ele class group $K^1$ is compact
  (Fujisaki's lemma, Weil~1974, Ch.~IV~\S2).
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Peter-Weyl on $K^1$]
  \label{thm:peter-weyl-adelic}
  \lean{GppHaar.open_peter_weyl_adelic_discrete_spectrum}
  \uses{lem:fujisaki}
  $L^2(K^1, d^\times a)$ decomposes into finite-dimensional irreducibles,
  so any left-invariant self-adjoint operator on $L^2(K^1)$ has discrete spectrum.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Functional Equation}

\section{Completed Riemann Zeta Function}

\begin{definition}[Riemann Xi Function]
  \label{def:xi}
  \lean{GppFE.riemannXi}
  \leanok
  $\xi(s) = \tfrac{1}{2} s(s-1) \pi^{-s/2} \Gamma(s/2) \zeta(s)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Tate Functional Equation]
  \label{thm:tate-fe}
  \lean{GppFE.open_tate_functional_equation}
  \uses{thm:adelic-haar}
  For any Schwartz-Bruhat function $\Phi$ on $\mathbb{A}$:
  $Z(\Phi, s) = Z(\hat\Phi, 1-s)$.
  Follows from Haar self-duality + Poisson summation over $\mathbb{Q} \subset \mathbb{A}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Functional Equation $\xi(s) = \xi(1-s)$]
  \label{thm:functional-equation}
  \lean{GppFE.completed_zeta_functional_eq}
  \uses{thm:tate-fe}
  $\xi(s) = \xi(1-s)$ for all $s \in \mathbb{C}$ away from poles.
  This is the arithmetic shadow symmetry.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[Zeros Are Symmetric]
  \label{lem:xi-zero-symmetric}
  \lean{GppFE.xi_zero_symmetric}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:functional-equation}
  If $\xi(\rho) = 0$ then $\xi(1-\rho) = 0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Critical Line is Fixed Locus]
  \label{lem:critical-line-fixed}
  \lean{GppFE.critical_line_is_fixed_locus}
  \leanok
  $s = 1-s \iff \mathrm{Re}(s) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.
\end{lemma}

\chapter{Shadow Symmetry = Time Reversal}

\section{The Three-Step Proof}

\begin{theorem}[Shadow is Involution]
  \label{thm:shadow-involution}
  \lean{GppShadow.shadow_is_involution}
  \leanok
  The map $\Delta \mapsto 2-\Delta$ has order~$2$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Penrose Antipodal from Hodge Star]
  \label{thm:penrose-antipodal}
  \lean{GppShadow.open_penrose_antipodal_from_hodge}
  \uses{thm:haar-self-dual}
  The orthogonal complement $\Lambda \mapsto \Lambda^\perp$ on $\mathrm{Gr}(2,4)$
  acts as the antipodal map on $S^2$ via the Penrose correspondence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Antipodal Forces Energy Inversion]
  \label{thm:energy-inversion}
  \lean{GppShadow.energy_inversion_from_antipodal}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:penrose-antipodal}
  Under $\Lambda \mapsto \Lambda^\perp$, the symplectic normalisation
  forces $\omega \mapsto \omega^{-1}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Shadow = Time Reversal]
  \label{thm:shadow-cpt}
  \lean{GppShadow.shadow_equals_time_reversal}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:energy-inversion}
  On the principal series $\Delta = 1+i\lambda$, time reversal $T$ sends
  $\Delta \mapsto 2-\Delta$: the shadow transform.
  (\emph{Most-cited result in On the Nature of Nature v5.2: 16 cross-references.})
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Canonical Dictionary $\Delta = 2s$]
  \label{thm:canonical-dict}
  \lean{GppShadow.delta_2s_shadow_is_functional_equation}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:shadow-cpt}
  Under $\Delta = 2s$, shadow $\Delta \leftrightarrow 2-\Delta$ is the
  functional equation $s \leftrightarrow 1-s$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Critical Lines Coincide]
  \label{thm:critical-lines}
  \lean{GppShadow.critical_lines_coincide}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:canonical-dict}
  $\mathrm{Re}(\Delta) = 1$ under $\Delta = 2s$ gives $\mathrm{Re}(s) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Zero Pairing and the Positivity Reduction (RH is not proved here)}

\section{Two-Zeros Argument}

\begin{lemma}[Functional Equation Gives Zero Companion]
  \label{lem:zeta-fe-zero}
  \lean{GppRH.zeta_zero_implies_fe_zero}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:functional-equation}
  If $\zeta(\rho) = 0$ then $\zeta(1-\rho) = 0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Companion Is Distinct Off Critical Line]
  \label{lem:companion-distinct}
  \lean{GppRH.companion_ne_of_off_critical}
  \leanok
  If $\mathrm{Re}(\rho) \neq \tfrac{1}{2}$ then $1-\bar\rho \neq \rho$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Two Zeros at Each Off-Critical Ordinate]
  \label{thm:two-zeros}
  \lean{GppRH.two_zeros_at_ordinate}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:zeta-fe-zero, lem:companion-distinct}
  If $\zeta(\rho) = 0$ with $\mathrm{Re}(\rho) \neq \tfrac{1}{2}$,
  there exist at least two distinct zeros with $\mathrm{Im}(s) = \mathrm{Im}(\rho)$.
  \emph{(Zero sorries, zero axioms.)}
\end{theorem}

\section{Temperedness}

\begin{theorem}[Temperedness $\Leftrightarrow$ Critical Line]
  \label{thm:temperedness}
  \lean{GppRH.temperedness_iff_critical_line}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:two-zeros}
  $e^{au}$ defines a tempered distribution on $\mathbb{R}$ iff $a=0$.
  Fully proved, no \texttt{sorry} and no axiom (the growth direction is
  \texttt{ExpNotTempered.exp\_growth\_not\_tempered}). This is a fact about a single
  exponential; it says nothing by itself about the zeros of $\zeta$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[RH reduces to Weil-pairing positivity (a conditional; RH is not claimed)]
  \label{thm:rh}
  \lean{GppWeilCriterion.rh_of_weil_pairedForm_nonneg}
  \uses{thm:two-zeros, thm:temperedness, thm:peter-weyl-adelic}
  If the Weil/Yakaboylu paired form is positive semidefinite on every finite
  subset of the nontrivial zero set, then every non-trivial zero of $\zeta(s)$
  satisfies $\mathrm{Re}(s) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.

  \emph{Status:} the former \texttt{arithmetic\_admissibility} axiom (which
  asserted RH verbatim) is retired. The conditional above is proved with no
  axioms beyond Mathlib's built-ins; the open analytic content is the
  positivity hypothesis (explicit formula / operator compression). That hypothesis is
  open and is not established anywhere in this tree, so this theorem does not prove RH.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Link 6 as a Named Hypothesis}

\begin{theorem}[Link 6: $c_{2D} = \kappa_0 \cdot c_{4D}^{\mathrm{Weyl}}$]
  \label{thm:link6}
  \lean{GppLink6.Link6Hypothesis}
  The celestial central charge $c_{2D}$ equals $\kappa_0$ times the Weyl anomaly
  $c_{4D}^{\mathrm{Weyl}}$.

  \textbf{Formalized:} the identity is \emph{not} asserted. It is carried as the
  explicit hypothesis \texttt{Link6Hypothesis}, so every consumer displays its
  dependence on Link~6 in its own statement rather than inheriting a global axiom.
  The five axioms this entry previously named (\texttt{link6\_from\_physics} plus
  Weinberg soft-graviton, Cachazo--Strominger celestial OPE, Capper--Duff one-loop
  Weyl anomaly, Adler--Bardeen non-renormalization) were retired on 2026-08-30;
  the four physics inputs survive as documented \texttt{open\_} placeholders.
  The algebraic corollary \texttt{link6\_corollary}
  ($c_{2D} = 0 \iff c_{4D}^{\mathrm{Weyl}} = 0$, given $\kappa_0 > 0$) is proved clean
  and is unconditional on Link~6 itself.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Three Generations from $c = 0$]
  \label{cor:three-gen-link6}
  \lean{GppLink6.three_generations_from_c0}
  \uses{thm:link6}
  $c_{2D} = 0 \Rightarrow c_{4D}^{\mathrm{Weyl}} = 0 \Rightarrow n_{\mathrm{gen}} = 3$
  (Boyle--Turok 2021).
\end{corollary}

\chapter{Celestial Holography}

\section{Shadow Discontinuity = Loop Integrand}

\begin{theorem}[Shadow Discontinuity]
  \label{thm:shadow-discontinuity}
  \lean{GppShadowDisc.open_shadow_discontinuity}
  \uses{thm:shadow-cpt}
  The discontinuity of a celestial amplitude across the shadow cut
  $z \mapsto \bar{z}$ (i.e., $\Delta \mapsto 2-\bar\Delta$) equals the loop integrand,
  replacing Feynman diagrams with analytic continuation.

  \textbf{Correction, re-audited 2026-08-15:} the cited declaration
  \texttt{GppShadowDisc.shadow\_discontinuity} is a \texttt{theorem foo : True := trivial}
  stub, not a proof of the statement above. The four ``proved clean'' facts below it
  \emph{are} real, kernel-checked theorems in \texttt{ShadowDiscontinuity.lean} --- but
  they are elementary complex-analysis/algebra lemmas the main claim would use as
  ingredients, not a proof of the claim itself. See
  Section~\ref{sec:tree-loop-sewing} for the concrete topology-level advance on this
  thread and the precise remaining analytic gap.

  \emph{Proved clean (as standalone lemmas, not as a proof of the boxed claim above):}
  \begin{itemize}
    \item $\mathrm{Disc}\,f(x) = 2i\,\mathrm{Im}\,f(x)$ (basic complex analysis).
    \item Shadow is an involution: $2-(2-s)=s$.
    \item Shadow equals conjugate on principal series $\Delta = 1+i\lambda$.
    \item Residue at simple pole: algebraic identity.
  \end{itemize}

  \emph{Gap:} celestial amplitude theory, unitarity cut equations, celestial OPE
  (not in Mathlib 4.19.0). Tracked honestly as three named stubs,
  \texttt{celestial\_amplitude\_has\_cut}, \texttt{disc\_equals\_loop\_integrand},
  \texttt{shadow\_disc\_mellin\_density} --- none discharged by this chapter.
\end{theorem}

\section{Tree-to-Loop Topology (Shadow-Pair Sewing)}
\label{sec:tree-loop-sewing}

From Toupin, \emph{Loop Integrands Hidden in Trees: Explicit Extraction by Double Shadow
Discontinuities} (Aug.\ 2026). The paper's central claim: a higher-point \emph{tree}
celestial correlator's shadow-pole analytic structure already encodes a lower-point loop
\emph{integrand}, extractable by a double shadow discontinuity. This section covers the
graph-theoretic layer of that claim, formalized in \texttt{GppTreeLoopSewing.lean} ---
unconditional, no axiom, no sorry --- and states precisely what remains open.

\begin{theorem}[Pair-Sewing Cycle Rank]
  \label{thm:pairsewing-cyclerank}
  \lean{GppTreeLoopSewing.pairSewing_cycleRank}
  \leanok
  For a connected cubic tree with $n = 4+2L$ external leaves ($V_T = n-2$ trivalent
  vertices, $I_T = n-3$ internal edges), sewing $L$ disjoint pairs among the designated
  $2L$ extra leaves leaves $4$ external legs and $I = I_T + L = 3L+1$ internal edges, with
  cycle rank $\beta_1 = I - V_T + 1 = L$. Proved for all $L$ by direct computation on the
  vertex/edge counts (\texttt{omega}); no combinatorial machinery beyond arithmetic is
  needed since only the counts, not an explicit graph object, are formalized here.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[One-Loop Box Specialization]
  \label{cor:sixpoint-oneloop}
  \lean{GppTreeLoopSewing.sixPoint_onePair_oneLoop_counts}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:pairsewing-cyclerank}
  At $L=1$: the open six-point cubic tree (4 vertices, 3 internal edges) with one pair
  sewing gives 4 external legs, 4 internal edges, cycle rank 1 --- the one-loop box
  topology.
\end{corollary}

\begin{lemma}[Box Denominator is the Closure Edge Times the Open Chain]
  \label{lem:box-denominator-closure}
  \lean{GppTreeLoopSewing.boxDenominator_is_pairClosure}
  \leanok
  With $D(x) := Q(x)$ the propagator-denominator function, the closed box denominator
  $Q(\ell)\,Q(\ell-p_1)\,Q(\ell-p_1-p_2)\,Q(\ell+p_4)$ is literally the missing closure
  edge $Q(\ell)$ times the three denominators already present in the open six-point
  chain. A definitional identity once the open-chain object is named.
\end{lemma}

\begin{definition}[Shadow-Pair Sewing Interface --- the Isolated Open Problem]
  \label{def:shadow-pair-sewing}
  \lean{GppTreeLoopSewing.ShadowPairSewing}
  A structure with fields \texttt{doubleDisc} (tree $\to$ pair shadow discontinuity),
  \texttt{inverseMellin}, \texttt{closePair} (the same closure described directly in
  momentum space), and a field \texttt{sewing\_identity} asserting
  \texttt{inverseMellin(doubleDisc T a b) = closePair T a b}. This is the paper's own
  boxed ``remaining analytic theorem''
  ($\mathcal M^{-1}_{5,6}[\mathrm{dDisc}_{\rm sh}^{(56)}\widetilde T_6] =
  \tfrac{i}{\ell^2+i0}T_6(\ell,p_1,\dots,-\ell)$), stated here as a \emph{local}
  hypothesis rather than a global axiom or an unnamed gap. Nothing in this file proves
  \texttt{sewing\_identity} for an explicit six-point celestial amplitude, and it is not
  claimed to be proved. \texttt{ShadowPairSewing.tree\_to\_loop\_extraction} is the
  (structurally trivial) corollary that the extraction pipeline commutes, conditional on
  this one named hypothesis.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Sewing Sign Opposition]
  \label{thm:shadow-sign-opposition}
  \lean{GppShadowSignOpposition.sign_opposition}
  \leanok
  Fix a celestial null 4-vector $q(x,y) = (1+x^2+y^2,\,2x,\,2y,\,1-x^2-y^2)$ (the real
  Lorentzian slice $\bar z = z^*$, $z=x+iy$) and the frame legs $p_1=(-E,0,0,E)$,
  $p_2=(-E,0,0,-E)$, $p_4=(E,-E\sin\theta,0,-E\cos\theta)$. Writing $A := 2q{\cdot}p_1$,
  $A' := 2q{\cdot}p_2$, $B := 2q{\cdot}(p_1{+}p_2)$, $C := 2q{\cdot}p_4$: $A = -4E$
  identically (no $z$-dependence), $A' = -4E|z|^2 \le 0$, $B = -4E(1+|z|^2) < 0$ for
  $E>0$, and $C$ clears its $(1-\cos\theta)$ factor to an exact sum of two squares,
  hence $C \ge 0$ for $\cos\theta < 1$ (i.e. $t \ne 0$). Consequently, for any physical
  t-channel threshold $B''>0$ and any point with $C \ne 0$ (away from the collinear
  point), the two tied-leg sewing coefficients $-1/(2ACB)$ and $-1/(2A'CB'')$ have a
  nonpositive product --- they are never both nonnegative and never both nonpositive.
  This is the exact algebraic content behind the numerical finding (checked at 39/39
  structured and 666/666 random kinematic points, zero exceptions, in
  \texttt{discovery/shadow\_ope/sign\_opposition\_sweep.py}) that the tied-leg
  discontinuities $\mathrm{Sewn}_s$, $\mathrm{Sewn}_t$ always carry opposite-sign
  imaginary parts. Pure real algebra and elementary geometry: no Mellin transforms, no
  complex analysis, no Legendre functions.
\end{theorem}

\emph{Honest boundary.} What is proved: the graph-combinatorial topology count
(Theorem~\ref{thm:pairsewing-cyclerank}), its one-loop specialization
(Corollary~\ref{cor:sixpoint-oneloop}), the denominator bookkeeping
(Lemma~\ref{lem:box-denominator-closure}), and the sewing sign-opposition fact
(Theorem~\ref{thm:shadow-sign-opposition}) --- all unconditional. What is \emph{not}
proved, and is isolated rather than hidden: the analytic celestial-sewing identity
(Definition~\ref{def:shadow-pair-sewing}), which requires an explicit six-point celestial
tree amplitude computation the paper itself states has not yet been carried out, and the
Sokhotski-Plemelj discontinuity construction itself (the actual $\mathrm{Sewn}_s$,
$\mathrm{Sewn}_t$, their $\lambda$-integral closed forms, and any comparison to the box
integral), which remains exploratory Python in \texttt{discovery/}, not formalized. This
section does not discharge, and does not claim to discharge,
\texttt{disc\_equals\_loop\_integrand} or the other two stubs in
Theorem~\ref{thm:shadow-discontinuity}.

\section{Loops from Cuts (canonical replacement series)}

Daniel has designated \texttt{Loops\_from\_Cuts\_in\_Celestial\_Holography.tex} et al.\ as
the canonical replacement for the shadow-discontinuity framing above: loop integrands arise
from an ordinary two-particle \emph{unitarity cut} on the celestial sphere, not a residue at
a shadow pole. See the Quantum Gravity --- Blackbody Law chapter for the spectral-weight
theorems already landed from this series; this section covers the loop paper's own most
novel link, the cut geometry itself.

\begin{theorem}[Antipodal Pairing --- Algebraic Core]
  \label{thm:antipodal-pairing-core}
  \lean{GppAntipodalPairing.antipodal_solves_conservation}
  \lean{GppAntipodalPairing.qVec_isNull}
  \leanok
  For the null celestial momentum direction $q(x,y) = (1{+}x^2{+}y^2,\,2x,\,2y,\,1{-}x^2{-}y^2)$
  (metric $(+,-,-,-)$; $\langle q(x,y),q(x,y)\rangle=0$ identically, for every $x,y$) and
  every $(x_5,y_5)\ne(0,0)$, $M\in\R$, writing $r^2:=x_5^2+y_5^2$,
  \[
    z_6 = -\tfrac{z_5}{r^2},\qquad
    \omega_5 = \tfrac{M}{2(1+r^2)},\qquad
    \omega_6 = \tfrac{M r^2}{2(1+r^2)},
  \]
  the resulting momenta satisfy $\omega_5\,q(x_5,y_5) + \omega_6\,q(x_6,y_6) = (M,0,0,0)$
  componentwise.
  \emph{Proved clean}: `Loops\_from\_Cuts\_in\_Celestial\_Holography.tex`, Theorem
  "Cut geometry: antipodal pairing and uniform measure" (`thm:measure`) --- the algebraic
  core of the paper's own claimed solution, verified here by direct vector computation
  (`field_simp`/`ring` on each of the four components after `fin_cases`), independent of and
  prior to any measure-theoretic argument.
  \emph{Not attempted}: uniqueness of this solution (a separate fact about the orbit
  structure of null directions on the two-sphere), and the phase-space \emph{measure}
  reduction itself,
  $\dd\Pi_2 = \dd^2z/[8\pi^2(1{+}|z|^2)^2]$, $\int\dd\Pi_2=1/(8\pi)$ --- this needs genuine
  $\delta^4$-constrained pushforward-measure and Jacobian machinery this repository has not
  built, and is left open as the natural next step of this thread.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Beta-Reflection Integral, Real Form]
  \label{thm:beta-reflection-real}
  \lean{GppDispersionKernel.beta_reflection_real}
  \leanok
  For $0<s<1$,
  \[
    \int_0^1 x^{s-1}(1-x)^{-s}\,\dd x \;=\; \frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi s)}.
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean}: the Beta-reflection integral underlying
  \texttt{Loops\_from\_Cuts\_in\_Celestial\_Holography.tex}'s dispersion-relation
  reconstruction (\texttt{thm:disp}, \texttt{thm:celdisp}), whose Mellin kernel
  $\int_0^\infty S^{\sigma-1}/(s'{+}S)\,\dd S = s'^{\sigma-1}\pi/\sin(\pi\sigma)$ reduces, via
  $S=s'u$, to the base case $\int_0^\infty u^{\sigma-1}/(1{+}u)\,\dd u=\pi/\sin(\pi\sigma)$ ---
  a ``second Euler Beta integral'' on $(0,\infty)$ confirmed absent from Mathlib v4.19.0 by
  direct grep (only the $(0,1)$ form, \texttt{Complex.betaIntegral}, exists). This theorem is
  \texttt{Complex.betaIntegral s (1-s)} unfolded to its defining real interval integral and
  evaluated via \texttt{Complex.Gamma\_mul\_Gamma\_eq\_betaIntegral} combined with the
  reflection formula \texttt{Complex.Gamma\_mul\_Gamma\_one\_sub}, cast down to $\R$ via
  \texttt{Complex.ofReal\_cpow} (valid uniformly on $x\in[0,1]$, both endpoints included) and
  \texttt{intervalIntegral.integral\_ofReal}.
  \emph{Not attempted}: the substitution $x=t/(1{+}t)$ mapping $(0,1)\leftrightarrow(0,\infty)$
  needed to reach the paper's actual $(0,\infty)$ dispersion kernel --- the natural tool is
  \texttt{MeasureTheory.integral\_image\_eq\_integral\_abs\_deriv\_smul} with a fresh
  $\mathrm{Ioo}\,0\,1\to\mathrm{Ioi}\,0$ diffeomorphism, genuine new infrastructure this
  repository has not built (the algebra was checked by hand, not yet coded), left open as the
  well-scoped next step.
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{Logistic Fourier pair (item 3 of 3).}
\texttt{Modular\_Thermality\_of\_the\_Celestial\_Spectral\_Weight.tex} and
\texttt{Spectral\_Weight\_from\_Principal\_Series.tex} also characterize $P(\lambda)$ (already
proved in closed hyperbolic form, $P(\lambda)=\pi\lambda/\sinh(\pi\lambda)$, in
Theorem~\ref{thm:planck-form}) as the Fourier transform of the logistic density.

\begin{theorem}[Logistic Fourier pair]
  \label{thm:logistic-fourier-pair}
  \lean{GppLogisticFourierPair.logistic_fourier_pair}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:planck-form}
  For every real $\lambda \neq 0$,
  \[
    \int_{-\infty}^{\infty} \frac{e^{i\lambda x}}{4\cosh^2(x/2)}\,\dd x
    \;=\; \frac{\pi\lambda}{\sinh(\pi\lambda)} .
  \]
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Logistic density has unit mass]
  \label{thm:logistic-density}
  \lean{GppLogisticFourierPair.integral_logistic_density}
  \leanok
  $\int_{-\infty}^{\infty} \frac{\dd x}{4\cosh^2(x/2)} = 1$, matching $P(0)=1$.
\end{theorem}

\textbf{The route.} Substituting the logistic distribution function $u = 1/(1+e^{-x})$ turns
the weight into $\dd u$ and the character $e^{i\lambda x}$ into $u^{i\lambda}(1-u)^{-i\lambda}$,
so the integral is the Beta integral $B(1+i\lambda,\,1-i\lambda) = \Gamma(1+i\lambda)\,
\Gamma(1-i\lambda)$, and Euler's reflection formula gives $\pi\lambda/\sinh(\pi\lambda)$.

\textbf{Why this sat as a stub.} From 2026-08 until 2026-09-26 this was a \texttt{True}-stub
recorded as out of reach: the pinned Mathlib (\texttt{v4.19.0}) had no \texttt{sech}, no
closed-form Fourier transform beyond the Gaussian, no Poisson-kernel pair, and no residue
calculus. That census was accurate --- \texttt{sech} still has zero occurrences at
\texttt{v4.33.1} --- but it listed the tools of the textbook residue proof without asking
whether another proof needed them. The Beta route uses none of them.

\textbf{What this does not give.} The First Moment Theorem needs the \emph{inverse} direction,
$\frac{1}{2\pi}\int P(\lambda)\cos(\lambda y)\,\dd\lambda = 1/(4\cosh^2(y/2))$. That follows by
Fourier inversion and is not proved here.

\section{Dispersion Reconstruction: the Mechanism Behind \texorpdfstring{\texttt{sewing\_identity}}{sewing\_identity}}
\label{sec:dispersion-reconstruction}

From a fresh session (2026-08-17) attacking Definition~\ref{def:shadow-pair-sewing}'s
open hypothesis head-on. \texttt{DispersionReconstruction.lean} proves the general,
physics-convention-independent complex-analysis mechanism --- the algebraic core of the
Sokhotski--Plemelj formula --- by which a discontinuity across a real pole determines the
meromorphic function having that pole. This is \emph{not} a celestial calculation: it is
the classical dispersion-relation fact
$F(z) = \tfrac{1}{2\pi i}\int_\R [\mathrm{Disc}\,F(x)]/(x-z)\,dx$ that Theorem
\ref{thm:shadow-discontinuity}'s own Step~6 invokes (via cut-constructibility) without
deriving.

\begin{theorem}[Exact Finite-\texorpdfstring{$\varepsilon$}{eps} Lorentzian Jump]
  \label{thm:lorentzian-jump}
  \lean{GppDispersion.lorentzian_jump}
  \leanok
  For real $z_0,\varepsilon,x$ with $\varepsilon\neq0$,
  $\dfrac{1}{(x-z_0)+i\varepsilon} - \dfrac{1}{(x-z_0)-i\varepsilon}
    = \dfrac{-2i\varepsilon}{(x-z_0)^2+\varepsilon^2}$, exactly, with no limiting
  procedure --- the finite-$\varepsilon$ content of ``the jump of a regulated simple pole
  is a Lorentzian kernel.''
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise Vanishing Off the Pole]
  \label{thm:lorentzian-vanish}
  \lean{GppDispersion.lorentzian_kernel_tendsto_zero_off_pole}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lorentzian-jump}
  Away from $x=z_0$, the Lorentzian kernel $\varepsilon/((x-z_0)^2+\varepsilon^2)$ tends to
  $0$ as $\varepsilon\to0^+$ --- the rigorous, non-distributional half of ``the regulated
  jump concentrates at the pole.'' The complementary mass statement
  $\int_\R \varepsilon/((x-z_0)^2+\varepsilon^2)\,dx = \pi$ for every $\varepsilon>0$ (a
  standard Cauchy/Poisson-kernel fact, reducible to Mathlib's
  \texttt{integral\_univ\_inv\_one\_add\_sq} by the affine substitution
  $x=z_0+\varepsilon u$) is numerically certified in \texttt{verify\_dispersion.py} but
  \emph{not} additionally formalized this session --- the substitution needs a
  translation-invariance-of-Lebesgue-measure lemma not chased down; named honestly as a
  gap rather than forced.
\end{theorem}

\emph{What this does and does not establish about \texttt{sewing\_identity}.} These two
theorems make precise which three analytic facts about the actual six-point celestial
tree would let \texttt{sewing\_identity} be \emph{derived} rather than assumed, via the
classical dispersion relation (valid for $F$ meromorphic off the real axis with a single
real simple pole and suitable decay --- Titchmarsh, \emph{Theory of Functions}, Ch.~5):
\textbf{(H1) Meromorphy} --- after the $\ell$-space completeness/inverse-Mellin integral
of $\mathrm{dDisc}_{\rm sh}^{(56)}\widetilde T_6$, the result is meromorphic in $\ell^2$
with its only singularity a simple pole at $\ell^2=0$; \textbf{(H2) Decay} --- that
function vanishes at infinity fast enough for the dispersion contour to close;
\textbf{(H3) Residue match} --- the discontinuity computed via the shadow-pair OPE (the
existing Steps~1--5 of Theorem~\ref{thm:shadow-discontinuity}) has, at $\ell^2=0$, exactly
the residue $-2\pi i\cdot T_6(\ell,p_1,\dots,p_4,-\ell)$ required. \textbf{None of
(H1)--(H3) is established here} for the actual $(z,\bar z)$-dependent celestial six-point
amplitude --- that is exactly the open boundary every paper in this program already
names. What is new is that the \emph{mechanism} (Sokhotski--Plemelj) is now a proved fact
in this tree instead of an implicit citation, and the remaining gap is three named,
checkable analytic properties of $G_6^{\rm tree}$ instead of one opaque hypothesis. This
section does not discharge \texttt{sewing\_identity} or touch any \texttt{GppShadowDisc}
stub.

\chapter{\texorpdfstring{Ad\`elic $L^2$}{Adelic L2} Regularization}

\begin{lemma}[Ad\`elic $L^2$ Regularization]
  \label{lem:adelic-l2-regularization}
  \lean{GppAdelicL2.open_adelic_l2_regularization}
  \uses{thm:l2-constraint, lem:fujisaki}
  The $L^2(\mathbb{A}^\times/\mathbb{Q}^\times)$ spectral decomposition is
  well-defined after Haar regularization:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item $K^1$ compact $\Rightarrow$ finite Haar measure $\Rightarrow$ $L^\infty \subseteq L^2$
    \item Peter-Weyl on $K^1$: $L^2(K^1) = \bigoplus_\chi \mathbb{C}\cdot\chi$
    \item Plancherel: $\|f\|^2 = \sum_\chi |\hat f(\chi)|^2$
  \end{enumerate}

  \emph{Algebraic core proved:} \texttt{l\_infty\_subset\_l2\_compact}
  (1 sorry: \texttt{integral\_mono} with bounded functions, Mathlib 4.19 gap).
  Three axioms: \texttt{peter\_weyl\_K1}, \texttt{plancherel\_K1},
  \texttt{spectrum\_discrete\_K1}.
\end{lemma}

\chapter{Spectral Weil Formula}

\begin{theorem}[Spectral Weil Closes Arithmetic Admissibility]
  \label{thm:spectral-weil}
  \lean{GppSpectralWeil.open_spectral_weil}
  \uses{lem:adelic-l2-regularization}
  The Weil explicit formula expresses $\sum_\rho h(\rho)$ in terms of local data.
  Meyer's (2005) spectral interpretation identifies the adèlic $L^2$ spectrum
  with the multiset of zeta zeros, closing \texttt{arithmetic\_admissibility}.

  \emph{Gap:} Weil explicit formula and Meyer's spectral-Weil identity
  require full adèlic Fourier analysis (not in Mathlib 4.19.0).
  Documented as three axioms: \texttt{weil\_explicit\_formula},
  \texttt{meyer\_spectral\_weil\_identity}, \texttt{weil\_distribution\_positivity}.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Ces\`aro Regularization and the Weil Positivity Criterion}

Results from the Ces\`aro/Abel-regularization papers and the finitely-supported
core of the Weil positivity criterion. Everything in this chapter is fully proved
in Lean with no axioms; the honest boundary (the operator theory of the intertwining
operator $\hat W$, and the analytic input that would discharge the positivity
hypothesis) is recorded in the module docs and deliberately \emph{not} claimed.

\begin{lemma}[Ces\`aro Mean Diverges off the Critical Line]
  \label{lem:cesaro-divergence}
  \lean{GppCesaroMean.tendsto_cesaro_mean_atTop_of_ne}
  \leanok
  For $\sigma \neq 1/2$, the symmetric Ces\`aro mean
  $(\int_{1/R}^{R} r^{2\sigma-2}\,dr)/(2\log R)$ tends to $+\infty$ as $R \to \infty$.
  Together with the exact value $1$ at $\sigma = 1/2$ (\texttt{born\_rule\_cesaro}),
  this makes $\sigma = 1/2$ the unique locus where the inversion-invariant mean is
  finite and nonzero.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Abel-Regularized Character Formula]
  \label{thm:abel-character}
  \lean{GppAbelCesaro.abel_character_formula_integral}
  \leanok
  For $|\alpha| < \varepsilon$:
  $\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{\mathbb R} e^{-\varepsilon|u|}e^{\alpha u}\,du
  = \varepsilon^2/(\varepsilon^2 - \alpha^2)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Unit Mass of the Regularized State]
  \label{cor:abel-state-one}
  \lean{GppAbelCesaro.abel_state_one_integral}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:abel-character}
  $\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{\mathbb R} e^{-\varepsilon|u|}\,du = 1$ exactly, for
  every $\varepsilon > 0$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[Periodic Eta Zeros]
  \label{thm:eta-periodic}
  \lean{GppCompletedEta.eta_factor_zero_iff}
  \leanok
  $2^{1-s} = 1$ iff $s = 1 - 2\pi i k/\log 2$ for some $k \in \mathbb Z$; every such
  zero has $\operatorname{Re} s = 1$
  (\texttt{GppCompletedEta.eta\_periodic\_zero\_re}), outside the open critical strip.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Regularized Matrix Element]
  \label{thm:yaka-element}
  \lean{GppYakaboylu.regularized_matrix_element}
  \leanok
  On the strip $1 - \varepsilon < \sigma < 1 + \varepsilon$:
  $\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\bigl(\int_0^1 t^{\sigma-2+\varepsilon}\,dt
  + \int_1^\infty t^{\sigma-2-\varepsilon}\,dt\bigr)
  = \varepsilon^2/(\varepsilon^2-(\sigma-1)^2)$, with value $1$ at $\sigma = 1$ and
  limit $0$ as $\varepsilon \to 0^+$ for $\sigma \neq 1$: the Kronecker-delta
  selection $\rho' = 1-\bar\rho$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite Positivity Criterion]
  \label{thm:weil-criterion}
  \lean{GppWeilCriterion.involution_fixed_of_pairedForm_nonneg}
  \leanok
  If $Z \subseteq \mathbb C$ is closed under an involution $\iota$ and the paired form
  $\sum_{\rho \in S} \overline{c(\iota\rho)}\,c(\rho)$ has nonnegative real part on
  every finite $S \subseteq Z$, then every point of $Z$ is fixed by $\iota$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Zero Set Closed under $\rho \mapsto 1-\bar\rho$]
  \label{thm:zeros-closed}
  \lean{GppWeilCriterion.nontrivialZeros_mem_involution}
  \leanok
  The nontrivial zero set of $\zeta$ is closed under $\rho \mapsto 1 - \bar\rho$ ---
  a proved theorem (functional equation composed with the proved conjugation
  symmetry), not an assumption.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[RH iff Finite Weil-Pairing Positivity]
  \label{thm:rh-iff-positivity}
  \lean{GppWeilCriterion.rh_iff_weil_pairedForm_nonneg}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:weil-criterion, thm:zeros-closed}
  Every nontrivial zero lies on the critical line \emph{iff} the paired form is
  positive semidefinite on every finite subset of the nontrivial zero set. A
  rigorous reduction --- not a proof of RH: the analytic input that would discharge
  the positivity hypothesis is not claimed.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The Eta Value as an Integral]
  \label{thm:sech-sq-integral}
  \lean{GppSechIntegral.integral_id_div_cosh_sq}
  \leanok
  $\int_0^\infty u/\cosh^2 u\,du = \log 2$ --- that is, $\eta(1) = \log 2$ in Mellin
  disguise, proved with no series interchange via the explicit antiderivative
  $u\tanh u - \log\cosh u$.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{L\textsuperscript{2} Constraint}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$L^2(K^1)$}{L²(K¹)} Unitarity and the Critical Line: what is proved and what is open}

\begin{lemma}[Shadow Fixed Points are Critical]
  \label{lem:shadow-fixed-critical}
  \lean{GppFE.critical_line_is_fixed_locus}
  \leanok
  For $s \in \mathbb{C}$, $\overline{s} = 1 - s \iff \mathrm{Re}(s) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.
  (The involution whose fixed locus is the critical line is $s \mapsto 1-\overline{s}$,
  not $s \mapsto 1-s$: the latter fixes only the single point $s = \tfrac12$.)
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[$L^2$ Unitarity Forces Critical Line]
  \label{lem:l2-unitarity}
  \lean{GppL2.conj_eq_shadow_iff_critical}
  \leanok
  If $\overline{s} = 1 - s$ then $\mathrm{Re}(s) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Principal Series Characters are Unitary]
  \label{lem:principal-series-unitary}
  \lean{GppL2.principal_series_char_unitary}
  \leanok
  For $s = \tfrac{1}{2} + i\gamma$, the character $a \mapsto |a|^{s-1/2}$
  has absolute value $1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[$L^2$ Constraint Theorem (open: a \texttt{True}-stub, not proved)]
  \label{thm:l2-constraint}
  \lean{GppL2.open_l2_constraint}
  \uses{lem:l2-unitarity, lem:fujisaki, thm:peter-weyl-adelic}
  If $\chi_s \in L^2(K^1)$ is an eigenfunction of the shadow involution
  $T: a \mapsto a^{-1}$, then $\mathrm{Re}(s) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.

  The proof assembles: Haar self-duality $\Rightarrow$ $T$ preserves $L^2$;
  Plancherel on $K^1$ $\Rightarrow$ $T$-eigenvalue is unitary;
  unitarity $\Rightarrow$ $\mathrm{Re}(s) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.

  Ad\`elic infrastructure (Hecke characters, Peter--Weyl on $K^1$)
  awaits \texttt{Mathlib.NumberTheory.NumberField.Adeles}. The Lean declaration is
  \texttt{open\_l2\_constraint : True}, a named placeholder with no content; only the
  elementary lemmas above (fixed locus, unitarity of principal-series characters) are proved.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Open: if a zero were $L^2$-admissible it would lie on the critical line]
  \label{cor:l2-rh}
  \lean{GppL2.open_l2_constraint_implies_rh}
  \uses{thm:l2-constraint}
  Every $L^2$-admissible non-trivial zero $\rho$ of $\zeta(s)$ satisfies
  $\mathrm{Re}(\rho) = \tfrac{1}{2}$.

  \textbf{Status: open, and not a proof of RH.} The Lean declaration is
  \texttt{open\_l2\_constraint\_implies\_rh : True}. No argument is given that the
  non-trivial zeros are $L^2$-admissible; supplying one is exactly the missing step.
\end{corollary}

\chapter{The Half-Flip: CPT, Time Reversal, and Complete Positivity}

The shadow involution squares to the identity on conformal dimensions. On \emph{states}
it does not: the antiunitary time-reversal operator $T$ squares to $-1$ on a
spin-$\tfrac12$ system. That factor of $-1$ --- the ``half-flip'' --- is what this
chapter formalizes, together with its consequence that the operation is not a physically
implementable channel.

\section{Wigner time reversal squares to $-1$}

\begin{definition}[Wigner time reversal on a qubit]
  \label{def:wignerT}
  \lean{GppHalfFlip.wignerT}
  \leanok
  $T(\psi_1,\psi_2) := (\overline{\psi_2},\, -\overline{\psi_1})$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[$T$ maps a state to an orthogonal one]
  \label{thm:wignerT-orthogonal}
  \lean{GppHalfFlip.wignerT_orthogonal}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:wignerT}
  $T\psi$ is orthogonal to $\psi$ for every $\psi$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The half-flip: $T^2 = -1$]
  \label{thm:wignerT-squared}
  \lean{GppHalfFlip.wignerT_wignerT}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:wignerT}
  $T(T\psi) = -\psi$. \emph{Proved clean.} This is the half-flip itself: applying time
  reversal twice returns the state up to a global sign, so $T$ generates a
  $\mathbb{Z}_4$, not a $\mathbb{Z}_2$, on spin-$\tfrac12$.
\end{theorem}

\section{Antiunitary conjugation is transposition}

\begin{theorem}[Hermitian conjugation acts as transpose]
  \label{thm:hermitian-star-transpose}
  \lean{GppHalfFlip.hermitian_map_star_eq_transpose}
  \leanok
  For a Hermitian matrix, entrywise complex conjugation coincides with transposition.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Antiunitary conjugation is unitary conjugation composed with transpose]
  \label{thm:antiunitary-transpose}
  \lean{GppHalfFlip.antiunitary_conj_eq_unitary_transpose}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:hermitian-star-transpose}
  This is the bridge from the physics statement (time reversal is antiunitary) to the
  matrix statement analysed below (time reversal involves a transpose).
\end{theorem}

\section{Transposition is positive but not completely positive}

The transpose preserves positive semidefiniteness, so on its own it looks like a legal
operation on states. It is not: tensoring with an untouched auxiliary system destroys
positivity. Formalized here without appeal to any physics input.

\begin{definition}[Complete positivity, general form]
  \label{def:completely-positive}
  \lean{GppChoiMatrix.CompletelyPositive}
  \leanok
  $\Phi$ is completely positive if $\Phi \otimes \mathrm{id}_\beta$ maps positive
  semidefinite matrices to positive semidefinite matrices for \emph{every} finite
  auxiliary dimension $\beta$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Choi criterion]
  \label{thm:choi-criterion}
  \lean{GppChoiMatrix.choiMatrix_posSemidef_of_completelyPositive}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:completely-positive}
  If $\Phi$ is completely positive then its Choi matrix is positive semidefinite.
  Proved for an \emph{arbitrary} finite-dimensional linear map, not just the transpose.
  Its contrapositive
  (\texttt{GppChoiMatrix.not\_completelyPositive\_of\_not\_posSemidef\_choiMatrix}) is
  the tool used below.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The Choi matrix of the transpose is SWAP]
  \label{thm:choi-transpose-swap}
  \lean{GppHalfFlipMatrix.choiMatrix_transposeMap_eq_SWAP}
  \leanok
  An exact finite computation: the same product of Kronecker deltas on both sides.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[SWAP is not positive semidefinite]
  \label{thm:swap-not-psd}
  \lean{GppHalfFlipMatrix.swap_not_posSemidef}
  \leanok
  The singlet $\psi$ satisfies $\mathrm{SWAP}\,\psi = -\psi$ with $\psi \neq 0$
  (\texttt{GppHalfFlipMatrix.SWAP\_mulVec\_psi},
  \texttt{GppHalfFlipMatrix.psi\_ne\_zero}), giving an explicit negative eigenvector.
  Note this is the strong statement --- \emph{not} positive semidefinite in Mathlib's own
  sense --- rather than merely ``has a negative eigenvalue''.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Transposition is not completely positive]
  \label{thm:transpose-not-cp}
  \lean{GppHalfFlipMatrix.transposeMap_not_completelyPositive}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:choi-criterion, thm:choi-transpose-swap, thm:swap-not-psd}
  \emph{Proved clean}, with no \texttt{sorry} and no axiom. Chaining the three results
  above: the Choi matrix of the transpose is SWAP, SWAP is not positive semidefinite, so
  by the Choi criterion the transpose is not completely positive.

  \textbf{Why this matters here.} By \ref{thm:antiunitary-transpose}, antiunitary time
  reversal carries a transpose. So the half-flip is not an artefact of a phase
  convention: the operation genuinely fails to be a channel, and the obstruction is the
  same negative eigenvector that makes the singlet antisymmetric.
\end{theorem}

\section{Entanglement bounds (same module family)}

\begin{theorem}[The CHSH value and its bounds]
  \label{thm:chsh}
  \lean{GppCHSHViolation.chshValue_exceeds_classical_bound}
  \leanok
  The constructed CHSH value equals $-1-\sqrt2$
  (\texttt{GppCHSHViolation.chshValue\_eq}), exceeds the classical bound $2$ in modulus,
  and respects Tsirelson's bound $2\sqrt2$
  (\texttt{GppCHSHViolation.chshValue\_within\_tsirelson\_bound}). All three proved
  clean.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Standard Model --- Three Generations}

\section{Division Algebra Tower}

\begin{lemma}[Hurwitz Classification]
  \label{lem:hurwitz}
  \lean{GppSM.HurwitzDimensionHypothesis}
  The only normed division algebras over $\mathbb{R}$ are
  $\mathbb{R}$, $\mathbb{C}$, $\mathbb{H}$, $\mathbb{O}$
  (Hurwitz 1898).  There are exactly $3$ Cayley--Dickson doublings.

  \textbf{Status:} carried as the \emph{hypothesis} \texttt{HurwitzDimensionHypothesis},
  not an axiom --- the former axiom was retired on 2026-08-30 because no theorem
  consumed it. The dimension-counting content this chapter actually uses
  (\texttt{cdStages\_card}, \texttt{exactly\_three\_doublings},
  \texttt{nda\_dimensions\_image}, \texttt{sedenion\_dim\_outside\_nda\_set})
  is proved outright, with no axiom.

  Gap: the real classification is not in Mathlib~4.19.0 (only the \emph{complex}
  Gelfand--Mazur theorem is). Note also that Mathlib's \texttt{NormedDivisionRing}
  extends \texttt{DivisionRing} and is therefore associative, so $\mathbb{O}$ does not
  inhabit it: as literally stated the $8$ case is vacuous and the reachable content is
  the Frobenius classification $\{1,2,4\}$. Any attempt to discharge this hypothesis
  should first restate it over a genuine composition-algebra structure.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Three Generations from Division Algebras]
  \label{cor:three-generations-anomaly}
  \lean{GppSM.open_three_generations}
  \uses{lem:hurwitz}
  The three Cayley--Dickson doublings $\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}$,
  $\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{H}$, $\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{O}$
  correspond to exactly $3$ fermion generations.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Anomaly Cancellation Forces 3 Generations]
  \label{thm:anomaly-cancel}
  \lean{GppSM.open_anomaly_cancellation_forces_three_generations}
  \uses{cor:three-generations-anomaly}
  With $c_{4D}^{\mathrm{Weyl}} = 0$ and SM gauge group,
  anomaly cancellation (Boyle--Turok 2021) forces
  $48 = 16 \times 3$ Weyl fermions, i.e., $n_{\mathrm{gen}} = 3$.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Standard Model --- Dark Matter Abundance}

\begin{theorem}[Dark Matter Abundance from Shadow Unitarity]
  \label{thm:dm-abundance}
  \lean{GppDM.dm_abundance_from_shadow}
  \uses{thm:link6}
  If $c_{2D} = 0$ (shadow unitarity), then the dark matter relic density
  $\Omega_{\mathrm{DM}} > 0$.

  \emph{Physics:} shadow symmetry breaking generates the DM mass scale;
  unitarity of the shadow amplitude fixes the abundance.
  Formalized via axioms \texttt{shadow\_breaking\_gives\_abundance}
  and \texttt{shadow\_unitarity\_abundance\_pos}.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Arithmetic and Quantum-Information Miscellanea (from On the Nature of Nature v5.2.1.3)}

Three small, self-contained results mined from the master manuscript
\texttt{On the Nature of Nature v5.2.1.3} in the 2026-08-19 session, none previously
formalized. Each is genuinely tied to real Mathlib infrastructure
(\texttt{riemannZeta}, its Euler product, or elementary trigonometry) —
none is restated bare-numeral arithmetic. Session note: this pass also
triaged large parts of \texttt{On the Nature of Nature v5.2.1.3} that were \emph{not}
formalized, either because the underlying claim was already covered by
an existing file (the bulk of the "Complete Arithmetic Table" and
Standard-Model chapters — see \texttt{DecodingReality.lean},
\texttt{WeylCasimir.lean}, \texttt{PerfectNumbersE8.lean},
\texttt{KappaShadow3.lean}, \texttt{ThreeGenerations.lean}, etc.) or
because the claim itself does not survive scrutiny (the "Path A" /
"Bridge" Riemann-Hypothesis section's Gram-matrix argument rests on a
ratio $\Lambda(1-s)/\Lambda(s)$ that the functional equation makes
identically $1$ for \emph{every} $s$, not a quantity that varies with
$\mathrm{Re}(s)$ as the argument requires) — the latter is recorded
privately in Supabase \texttt{research\_notes}, per this project's
standing rule against cataloguing manuscript errors in the public tree.

\begin{theorem}[Spin-Statistics Eta/Zeta Ratio]
  \label{thm:spin-stats-eta}
  \lean{GppSpinStatisticsEta.eta_div_zeta_two_mul_nat}
  \leanok
  For every $n \geq 1$, the Dirichlet eta function
  $\eta(s) = (1-2^{1-s})\zeta(s)$ satisfies
  $\eta(2n)/\zeta(2n) = 1 - 2^{1-2n}$, unconditionally (requires
  $\zeta(2n) \neq 0$, from \texttt{riemannZeta\_ne\_zero\_of\_one\_le\_re}).
  The concrete instance $\eta(4)/\zeta(4) = 7/8$
  (\texttt{eta\_four\_div\_zeta\_four'}) and the explicit value
  $\eta(4) = 7\pi^4/720$ (\texttt{eta\_four\_eq}, via
  \texttt{riemannZeta\_four}) are also proved.

  \emph{Honest boundary:} the physical identification of this ratio with
  a fermion/boson thermal-capacity ratio in $3{+}1$ dimensions is not
  formalized — only the arithmetic value.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Squarefree-Density Euler Product]
  \label{thm:squarefree-density}
  \lean{GppSquarefreeDensity.squarefree_density_partial_products_tendsto_six_div_pi_sq}
  \leanok
  The partial products $\prod_{p < n}(1-p^{-2})$ over primes converge to
  $6/\pi^2 = 1/\zeta(2)$, obtained by inverting Mathlib's convergent
  Euler product $\prod_{p<n}(1-p^{-2})^{-1} \to \zeta(2)$
  (\texttt{riemannZeta\_eulerProduct}) term-by-term and evaluating via
  \texttt{riemannZeta\_two}.

  \emph{Honest boundary:} the physical identification
  $\alpha/\pi^2 = (\alpha/6)\prod_p(1-p^{-2})$ with the fine-structure
  constant is not formalized — only the underlying arithmetic identity
  both rest on.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[CHSH Bell Violation and CKW Monogamy]
  \label{thm:chsh-violation}
  \lean{GppCHSHViolation.chshValue_exceeds_classical_bound}
  \leanok
  At the angle configuration $\theta_{ab}=0,\ \theta_{ab'}=\pi/2,\
  \theta_{a'b}=\theta_{a'b'}=\pi/4$, the CHSH combination
  $S = E(a,b)-E(a,b')+E(a',b)+E(a',b')$ with $E(\theta)=-\cos\theta$
  evaluates exactly to $-1-\sqrt2$
  (\texttt{chshValue\_eq}), so $|S| = 1+\sqrt2 > 2$, exceeding the
  classical bound, while respecting Tsirelson's bound $|S|\le 2\sqrt2$
  (\texttt{chshValue\_within\_tsirelson\_bound}). Separately,
  \texttt{ckw\_forces\_zero\_concurrence} formalizes the elementary
  consequence of the CKW monogamy inequality: given
  $1+x^2 \le y^2 \le 1$, necessarily $x=0$.

  \emph{Honest boundary:} this is standard textbook CHSH/CKW material,
  not GPP-specific; the source's stronger claim that Haar measure on
  $\mathrm{Gr}(2,4)$ \emph{forces} exactly the Tsirelson bound is not
  formalized, nor is the general quantum correlation-function formalism
  — only the source's own explicit numerical evaluation.
\end{theorem}

\chapter{Quantum Gravity --- Born Rule from Haar}

\begin{lemma}[Born Rule from Haar Measure]
  \label{lem:born-rule-haar}
  \lean{GppBorn.open_born_rule_from_haar}
  \uses{thm:adelic-haar, lem:fujisaki}
  The Born rule $P(\psi) = |\langle\phi|\psi\rangle|^2$ arises as the unique
  probability measure on $L^2(K^1)$ induced by Haar measure and
  Gleason's theorem.

  \emph{Proved clean:} Haar measure gives a probability measure on $K^1$;
  $\|f\|^2 = \int|f|^2 d\mu$ normalizes to $1$.
  \emph{Axioms:} \texttt{K1\_haar\_probability}, \texttt{born\_from\_haar},
  \texttt{gleason\_uniqueness} (Gleason 1957, not in Mathlib).
\end{lemma}

\chapter{Quantum Gravity --- Celestial Spectral Weight and All-Loop Bound}

From ``The Spectral Weight $\pi\lambda/\sinh\pi\lambda$'' and ``Modular Thermality of the
Celestial Spectral Weight'' (Toupin, 2026). $P(\lambda) := \pi\lambda/\sinh(\pi\lambda)$ is
the two-particle massless phase-space weight of a celestial unitarity cut: it is the Mellin
image $\Gamma(\Delta_5)\Gamma(\Delta_6)/\Gamma(\Delta_5+\Delta_6)$ of two-particle phase space,
restricted to the shadow locus $\Delta_5+\Delta_6=2$, $\Delta_5=1+i\lambda$, where it equals
$\Gamma(1+i\lambda)\Gamma(1-i\lambda)$.

\begin{theorem}[Stefan--Boltzmann Family]
  \label{thm:stefan-boltzmann-family}
  \lean{GppStefanBoltzmann.stefan_boltzmann_family}
  \leanok
  For every real $s > 0$,
  \[
    m_s \;:=\; \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_0^\infty \lambda^{s-1} P(\lambda)\,d\lambda
    \;=\; \pi^{-(s+1)}\bigl(1-2^{-(s+1)}\bigr)\Gamma(s+1)\zeta(s+1).
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean, for all real $s>0$}, not merely the sampled $s=1,2,3$: a
  $\pi$-substitution reduces the integral to the pre-existing zeta-bridge identity of
  \S\,Thread~S (\texttt{SinhZetaBridge.sinh\_mellin\_zeta}) at exponent $s+1$. Corollaries
  give $m_1 = 1/8$ and $m_3 = 1/16$ exactly.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Gamma-Modulus Identity]
  \label{thm:gamma-modulus}
  \lean{GppGammaModulus.gamma_one_add_mul_gamma_one_sub}
  \leanok
  For every real $\lambda \neq 0$,
  \[
    \Gamma(1+i\lambda)\,\Gamma(1-i\lambda) \;=\; \frac{\pi\lambda}{\sinh(\pi\lambda)} \;=\; P(\lambda).
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean}: Euler's reflection formula
  ($\Gamma(z)\Gamma(1-z) = \pi/\sin(\pi z)$, unconditional in Mathlib) shifted by one factor
  of $i\lambda$, with $\sin(x\cdot i) = i\sinh(x)$ turning the denominator real.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Ladder Convolution Bound]
  \label{thm:all-loop-finiteness}
  \lean{GppAllLoopFiniteness.finiteness}
  \leanok
  For the $L$-fold chain convolution of $P$ against itself,
  \[
    \mathcal{M}_L \;=\; \frac{1}{(2\pi)^L}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{>0}^L}
      \Bigl[\prod_{j=1}^L P(\lambda_j)\Bigr]\Bigl[\prod_{j=1}^{L-1}P(|\lambda_j-\lambda_{j+1}|)\Bigr]
      \,d\lambda_1\cdots d\lambda_L,
  \]
  and for every $L \geq 1$: $0 < \mathcal{M}_L \leq (1/8)^L$. This is a statement about the
  convolution integral $\mathcal{M}_L$ itself, not a claim about any physical loop amplitude.

  \emph{Proved clean, for every $L$ at once} (not instantiated at small $L$): the $L$-fold
  integral is encoded via a recursively-defined chain kernel realizing Fubini/Tonelli's own
  iterated-integral expansion of $\mathcal{M}_L$ (peeling off one loop variable, its external
  weight, and its connecting rung, at a time), carried entirely in the extended
  nonnegative reals so Tonelli's theorem and monotonicity of the integral are unconditional
  --- exactly mirroring the paper's own proof, which bounds every rung $P(|\lambda_j-\lambda_{j+1}|)$
  by $1$, discards it, and factorizes what remains via $\int_0^\infty P(\lambda)d\lambda = \pi/4$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Kinematic-Block Zeta Bridge]
  \label{thm:kinematic-zeta-bridge}
  \lean{GppKinematicBlock.zeta_bridge_kappa}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:stefan-boltzmann-family}
  With $\kappa(t) := (2\sinh t)^{-1}$, for every real $s > 1$,
  \[
    \int_0^\infty t^{s-1}\kappa(t)\,dt \;=\; \bigl(1-2^{-s}\bigr)\Gamma(s)\zeta(s).
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean}: `kinematic\_block\_v1.tex`, Proposition \texttt{prop:zetabridge}(a),
  at the paper's own kernel normalization --- a direct corollary of the pre-existing
  Thread~S zeta bridge (the leading factor of $2$ there cancels $\kappa$'s own $1/2$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Weierstrass Product for \texorpdfstring{$\sinh$}{sinh}]
  \label{thm:sinh-weierstrass}
  \lean{GppSinhWeierstrass.tendsto_prod_one_add_sq_div}
  \leanok
  For every real $\lambda$,
  \[
    \sinh(\pi\lambda) \;=\; \pi\lambda \prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\Bigl(1+\frac{\lambda^2}{n^2}\Bigr),
  \]
  as a genuine infinite product (the partial products converge).
  \emph{Proved clean}: \texttt{blackbody\_law\_qg\_dtoupin\_v1.tex}, Test T4 --- stronger
  than the paper's own numerical certification, which truncates at $N=2000$ with a
  Hurwitz-zeta tail bound. Derived from Mathlib's Euler product for $\sin$ via the
  substitution $z=i\lambda$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The Cumulant Law]
  \label{thm:cumulant-law}
  \lean{GppCumulantLaw.cumulant_law}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:sinh-weierstrass}
  For every real $\lambda$ with $|\lambda|<1$,
  \[
    -\log P(\lambda) \;=\; \sum_{k=0}^{\infty} (-1)^k\,\zeta(2(k+1))\,\frac{\lambda^{2(k+1)}}{k+1},
  \]
  equivalently $\log P(\lambda) = -\sum_{k\ge1}(-1)^{k+1}\zeta(2k)\lambda^{2k}/k$.
  \emph{Proved clean}: \texttt{blackbody\_law\_qg\_dtoupin\_v1.tex}, Test T5 ("cumulants are
  even zeta values"). Derived by taking $\log$ of the Weierstrass product
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:sinh-weierstrass}), expanding each factor's $\log(1+x)$ as its Taylor
  series, and swapping the resulting double sum (joint summability from an explicit product
  majorant).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Planck Form]
  \label{thm:planck-form}
  \lean{GppSpectralWeight.planck_form}
  \leanok
  For every real $\lambda > 0$, with $n_B(y) := 1/(e^y-1)$ the Bose occupation number,
  \[
    P(\lambda) \;=\; 2\pi\lambda\bigl(n_B(\pi\lambda) - n_B(2\pi\lambda)\bigr).
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean}: the zero-point contributions of the two Bose terms cancel exactly,
  leaving a genuine Planck-form identity for $P$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Reciprocal Weierstrass Product]
  \label{thm:one-div-p-weierstrass}
  \lean{GppSpectralWeight.one_div_P_tendsto_tprod}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:sinh-weierstrass}
  For every real $\lambda \neq 0$, the partial products of $1+\lambda^2/n^2$ converge to
  $1/P(\lambda)$.
  \emph{Proved clean}: an immediate corollary of Theorem~\ref{thm:sinh-weierstrass}.
\end{theorem}

\textbf{Honest boundary.} Not formalized from this upload: the rationality/PSLQ program
beyond $M_1, M_2$ (already covered by the pre-existing Thread~S/Thread~E); the
Wiener--Hopf/Parseval odd-zeta cancellation and wall-count combinatorics; Proposition
\texttt{prop:zetabridge}(b)'s analytic continuation to $\mathrm{Re}\,s>-1$; and most of the
blackbody capstone's remaining structural theorems (T1, T3, T6, T13, T14) beyond the
Gamma-modulus, Stefan--Boltzmann, Weierstrass-product, cumulant-law, Planck-form, and
reciprocal-product faces proved here; the weight-shift ODE for $P$'s Fourier transform
(real analysis, no digamma needed, but not yet set up); and the digamma-moment theorem and
Matsubara residues, both scoped in \texttt{docs/FORMALIZATION\_PLAN.md}.
Two gaps are blocking further progress and are recorded precisely in
\texttt{docs/FORMALIZATION\_PLAN.md} rather than attempted as rushed partial versions:
Mathlib \texttt{v4.19.0} has \emph{no digamma or polygamma function at all} (blocking the
First Moment Theorem, \texttt{thm:moment}), and \emph{no Legendre or conical special
functions} (blocking the entire kinematic-block/conical-function program: conical
reduction, shadow=Legendre-degree symmetry, the Mehler--Fock pair, the Temperedness
Theorem).

\begin{theorem}[Planck Form of the Plancherel Weight]
  \label{thm:planck-form-plancherel}
  \lean{GppPlanckForm.planck_form_bose_difference}
  \leanok
  For every real $\lambda \neq 0$, with $n_B(y) := (e^y-1)^{-1}$ the Bose--Einstein
  occupation number,
  \[
    P(\lambda) \;=\; 2\pi\lambda\,\bigl[n_B(\pi\lambda) - n_B(2\pi\lambda)\bigr].
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean}: from \texttt{Modular\_Thermality\_of\_the\_Celestial\_Spectral\_
  Weight.tex} and \texttt{Spectral\_Weight\_from\_Principal\_Series.tex} (identical
  statement in both) --- the papers Daniel has designated as the canonical replacements
  for the earlier haar-QG/kinematic-block/blackbody series. Pure hyperbolic algebra:
  $n_B(y) - n_B(2y) = 1/(2\sinh y)$ from $e^{2y}-1=(e^y-1)(e^y+1)$, no Mathlib gap. The
  paper's two other equivalent forms (the oscillator-sum series and the
  $E(x)=(x/2)\coth(x/2)$ zero-point-cancellation form) are not separately formalized:
  Mathlib has no \texttt{coth} function, and the oscillator sum is definitionally the
  same geometric series already inside \texttt{Real.sinh}'s own definition.
\end{theorem}

\textbf{Canonical-source update (2026-08-23).} Daniel has designated four new papers ---
\texttt{Loops\_from\_Cuts\_in\_Celestial\_Holography.tex},
\texttt{Principal\_Series\_Kinematic\_Blocks.tex},
\texttt{Spectral\_Weight\_from\_Principal\_Series.tex}, and
\texttt{Modular\_Thermality\_of\_the\_Celestial\_Spectral\_Weight.tex} --- as replacing the
earlier haar\_qg/kinematic\_block/blackbody series for the ``loop'', ``measure'', ``block'',
and ``blackbody'' roles respectively. The new loop paper's central correction: loop
integrands arise from an ordinary two-particle \emph{unitarity cut} on the celestial sphere
(antipodal pairing, Mellin image $\Gamma(\Delta_5)\Gamma(\Delta_6)/\Gamma(\Delta_5+\Delta_6)$,
dispersion reconstruction), \textbf{not} from a ``residue at the shadow pole'' $\Delta_5+
\Delta_6=2$ --- the new paper explicitly retracts that framing, identifying $\Delta_5+
\Delta_6=2$ instead as the locus of scale invariance of the cut, with no residue taken
anywhere. None of that unitarity-cut/dispersion machinery (Theorems \texttt{thm:measure},
\texttt{thm:mellincut}, \texttt{thm:boxcut}, \texttt{thm:poles}, \texttt{thm:disp},
\texttt{thm:celdisp} of the new loop paper) is formalized yet --- it needs new phase-space/
Jacobian infrastructure this repo does not currently have. The new kinematic-block paper adds
one genuinely new closed result on top of the pre-existing content (still blocked by the
missing-digamma/Legendre gaps above): \texttt{thm:ode}/\texttt{thm:resolved} prove, rather
than merely numerically observe, that the digamma first moment and the ladder moment
$\mathcal M_1$ are the \emph{same} quantity (both equal $\hat p(0)/2$ for the Fourier
partner $\hat p$ of $P$) via a first-order ODE for $\hat p$ --- still gated on the same
missing digamma function.

\begin{theorem}[Matsubara Pole Residues]
  \label{thm:matsubara-poles}
  \lean{GppMatsubara.tendsto_residue_at_matsubara}
  \leanok
  For every integer $n$, the complex-analytic continuation
  $P_{\mathbb C}(z) := \pi z/\sinh(\pi z)$ of the Plancherel weight has, at $z = in$,
  \[
    \lim_{\varepsilon\to0}\ \varepsilon\cdot P_{\mathbb C}(in+\varepsilon)
    \;=\; i\,(-1)^n\,n .
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean}: \texttt{Modular\_Thermality\_of\_the\_Celestial\_Spectral\_Weight.tex},
  Proposition "Equivalent descriptions" item (iv). \texttt{Res} is not invoked as a named
  Mathlib operator (no general residue-calculus API exists at the pinned commit); the
  residue is instead the literal punctured-neighborhood limit the paper's own script
  evaluates numerically, formalized here as a genuine \texttt{Tendsto} statement. Proof:
  $\sinh(\pi(in+\varepsilon)) = (-1)^n\sinh(\pi\varepsilon)$ (addition formula plus
  $\sinh(\pi in)=0$, $\cosh(\pi in)=(-1)^n$), and $P_{\mathbb C}(\varepsilon)\to1$ as
  $\varepsilon\to0$ from \texttt{Complex.hasDerivAt\_sinh} via the chain rule.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Log of the Sinh Weierstrass Product]
  \label{thm:sinh-log-series}
  \lean{GppSinhLogSeries.hasSum_log_one_add_sq_div}
  \leanok
  For every real $\lambda \neq 0$,
  \[
    \sum_{n=0}^{\infty} \log\!\Bigl(1+\frac{\lambda^2}{(n+1)^2}\Bigr)
    \;=\; \log\!\Bigl(\frac{\sinh(\pi\lambda)}{\pi\lambda}\Bigr),
  \]
  as a genuine unconditional \texttt{HasSum}, not a numerically-truncated approximation.
  \emph{Proved clean}: the first half of the cumulant-law chain (item (v) of the "Six
  faces"/"Equivalent descriptions" proposition, $\log P(\lambda) = -\sum(-1)^{k+1}\zeta(2k)
  \lambda^{2k}/k$) --- from \texttt{thm:sinh-weierstrass} above, dividing by the nonzero
  constant $\pi\lambda$, applying \texttt{Real.log} (continuous at the limit, proved
  positive here from \texttt{Real.sinh}'s strict monotonicity), and upgrading the resulting
  convergent partial sums of logs to a genuine \texttt{HasSum} via nonnegativity of every
  term. \emph{Not attempted}: expanding each $\log(1+x)$ into its own power series and
  swapping the resulting double sum to reach the paper's closed $\zeta(2k)$ form --- the
  genuinely new remaining work, scoped precisely in \texttt{docs/FORMALIZATION\_PLAN.md}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Weight-Shift Relations]
  \label{thm:weight-shift}
  \lean{GppWeightShift.shift_sub_I}
  \lean{GppWeightShift.shift_add_I}
  \leanok
  For the Gamma-product continuation $P_{\mathbb C}(z) := \Gamma(1+iz)\Gamma(1-iz)$ and
  every $z\ne0$ with $1\pm iz\ne0$ (automatic for real $z$),
  \[
    P_{\mathbb C}(z-i) = \frac{1+iz}{-iz}\,P_{\mathbb C}(z),
    \qquad
    P_{\mathbb C}(z+i) = \frac{1-iz}{iz}\,P_{\mathbb C}(z).
  \]
  \emph{Proved clean}: `Principal\_Series\_Kinematic\_Blocks.tex`, Theorem "Weight-shift
  relations and the resulting differential equation" --- the digamma-free first half (the
  shift relations themselves), a pure consequence of `Complex.Gamma\_add\_one`
  (`Γ(s+1)=sΓ(s)`) applied to each Gamma factor. The theorem's second half --- the
  resulting first-order ODE for `P`'s Fourier partner, needed for `thm:resolved`'s
  digamma-moment identification --- remains blocked on Mathlib's missing digamma function.
\end{theorem}

\texttt{thm:planck-form}, \texttt{thm:matsubara-poles}, \texttt{thm:sinh-log-series},
\texttt{thm:cumulant-law}, and \texttt{thm:weight-shift}
above are, so far, the five pieces from this new canonical series formalized with no
Mathlib blocker.

\chapter{General Relativity --- Rigidity}

\begin{theorem}[Einstein Uniqueness from Shadow Symmetry]
  \label{thm:rigidity}
  \lean{GppRigidity.open_einstein_uniqueness_from_shadow}
  \uses{thm:shadow-cpt, thm:link6}
  The Einstein field equations $G_{\mu\nu} = 8\pi G T_{\mu\nu}$ are uniquely
  determined by:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item Shadow symmetry $\Delta \mapsto 2-\Delta$ (diffeomorphism covariance),
    \item Two-derivative truncation ($c_{4D}^{\mathrm{Weyl}} = 0$),
    \item Positive energy (Weyl anomaly positivity).
  \end{enumerate}

  \emph{Algebraic core proved:}
  \texttt{graviton\_shadow\_dimension}: $(2:\mathbb{Z}) - 2 = 0$ (by \texttt{decide});
  \texttt{lovelock\_uniqueness\_algebraic}: trivial stub.

  \emph{Axioms:} \texttt{lovelock\_theorem} (Lovelock 1971),
  \texttt{shadow\_forces\_massless\_graviton},
  \texttt{c0\_eliminates\_higher\_curvature}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Cosmological Constant Unique]
  \label{cor:lambda-unique}
  \lean{GppRigidity.open_cosmological_constant_unique}
  \uses{thm:rigidity}
  Dark energy (cosmological constant $\Lambda$) is the only free parameter
  in the gravitational action consistent with shadow symmetry.
\end{corollary}

\chapter{Thread HT --- Prime--Archimedean Heat Trace (Elementary Layer)}

From \texttt{arithmetic\_principal\_series\_RH\_program34.tex} (the BPY prime--Archimedean
spectral program). That paper reformulates RH as a zero-independent statement: with
$m_*(u) = \tfrac{1}{2\sqrt{1+u}}(\xi'/\xi)(\tfrac12+\sqrt{1+u})$, $\mathcal{W}=\nu_\infty-\nu_p$
the completed prime--Archimedean boundary distribution, and
$\mathcal{K}(t)=(4\pi t)^{-1/2}\langle\mathcal{W},e^{-(\cdot)^2/(4t)}\rangle$, subordination of
the massive resolvent gives $m_*(u)=\int_0^\infty e^{-(1+u)t}\mathcal{K}(t)\,dt$, and the
paper's heat-trace criterion states
$\mathrm{RH} \iff \mathcal{K}$ is completely monotone on $(0,\infty)$.

This chapter covers the elementary, kernel-checkable layer of that chain: the arithmetic and
Laplace-transform bookkeeping. Two genuinely hard inputs (Bernstein/Hausdorff--Widder for the
criterion itself, and the digamma piece of the Archimedean Laplace transform) are named and
\emph{not} claimed here --- see the honest-boundary note at the end of this chapter.

\section{Complete Monotonicity and Laplace Bookkeeping}

\begin{definition}[Completely Monotone]
  \label{def:completely-monotone}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.CompletelyMonotone}
  \leanok
  $f$ is completely monotone on $(0,\infty)$ if $(-1)^n f^{(n)}(t) \geq 0$ for all $n$ and
  all $t>0$. Absent from Mathlib at the pinned commit \texttt{c44e0c8} --- no
  Bernstein/Hausdorff--Widder theory exists upstream.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Single Heat Mode is Completely Monotone]
  \label{lem:completely-monotone-exp-neg}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.completelyMonotone_exp_neg}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:completely-monotone}
  $t \mapsto e^{-at}$ is completely monotone for $a \geq 0$. In the paper's notation this is
  one term of $\mathcal{K}(t)=\sum_{\gamma>0} m_\gamma e^{-\gamma^2 t}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Elementary Resolvent Laplace Transform]
  \label{lem:resolvent-laplace}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.resolvent_laplace}
  \leanok
  $\int_0^\infty e^{-ct}\,dt = 1/c$ for $c>0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Shifted Resolvent Laplace Transform]
  \label{lem:laplace-resolvent-shift}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.laplace_resolvent_shift}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:resolvent-laplace}
  $\int_0^\infty e^{-(1+u)t}e^{-\gamma^2 t}\,dt = 1/(u+1+\gamma^2)$. The paper's displayed
  step converting the Hadamard partial-fraction sum into the heat expansion, by uniqueness
  of the Laplace transform.
\end{lemma}

\section{Subordination}

\begin{theorem}[Subordination at $x=0$]
  \label{thm:subordination-at-zero}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.subordination_at_zero}
  \leanok
  $\int_0^\infty e^{-r^2t}(4\pi t)^{-1/2}\,dt = 1/(2r)$ for $r>0$. The $x=0$ case of the
  paper's boxed identity $e^{-rx}/(2r) = \int_0^\infty e^{-r^2t}(4\pi t)^{-1/2}e^{-x^2/(4t)}\,dt$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Subordination, General $x$ --- $K_{1/2}$ Bessel Evaluation]
  \label{thm:subordination-general}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.subordination_general}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:subordination-at-zero}
  For $a,b>0$: $\int_0^\infty t^{-1/2}e^{-at-b/t}\,dt = \sqrt{\pi/a}\,e^{-2\sqrt{ab}}$. This
  is a genuine $K_{1/2}$ Bessel evaluation, confirmed absent from Mathlib entirely at the
  pin, proved here \emph{without any Bessel-function machinery}: the substitution
  $t=(\sqrt{b}/\sqrt{a})w^2$ reduces it to an auxiliary integral
  $\kappa(c) := \int_0^\infty e^{-c(w^2+w^{-2})}\,dw$; the $w\mapsto 1/w$ symmetry of the
  integrand doubles it to $2\kappa(c)=\int_0^\infty(1+w^{-2})e^{-c(w^2+w^{-2})}\,dw$; the
  substitution $p=w-1/w$ --- a bijection $(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}$ whose derivative is
  exactly the weight $1+w^{-2}$ --- turns this into a two-sided Gaussian integral, closed by
  \texttt{Real.integral\_gaussian}. This is the general-$x$ instance of the paper's boxed
  subordination formula, in full.
\end{theorem}

\section{Bridge to Thread L, and the Archimedean Laplace Transform}

\begin{theorem}[Thread L Bridge: Heat Gaussian Prime Side]
  \label{thm:prime-side-heat-gaussian}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.primeSide_heatGaussian}
  \leanok
  $\sum_{n\geq 2}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}e^{-(\log n)^2/(4t)}$ is exactly one half of
  \texttt{GppWeilLadder.primeSide} evaluated at the heat Gaussian
  $x\mapsto e^{-x^2/(4t)}$, since that test function is even. Consequence: the whole
  support-ladder toolkit of \texttt{WeilSupportLadder.lean} applies verbatim to the heat
  trace's prime side --- the two threads are the same object.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[Archimedean Laplace, First Elementary Sub-identity]
  \label{lem:archimedean-laplace-aux-one}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.archimedeanLaplace_aux_one}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:resolvent-laplace}
  For $r>-1/2$: $\int_0^\infty(e^{-rx}-e^{-x})e^{-x/2}\,dx = 1/(r+1/2)-2/3$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Archimedean Laplace, Second Elementary Sub-identity]
  \label{lem:archimedean-laplace-aux-two}
  \lean{GppHeatTrace.archimedeanLaplace_aux_two}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:resolvent-laplace}
  For $r>1/2$: $\int_0^\infty(e^{-rx}-e^{-x})e^{x/2}\,dx = 1/(r-1/2)-2$. Together with
  Lemma~\ref{lem:archimedean-laplace-aux-one}, these are the two purely elementary pieces of
  the paper's Archimedean Laplace transform $A_\infty(r)$; the remaining digamma piece is
  \textbf{not} formalized here. (Mathlib now supports a real digamma function
  $\psi=\Gamma'/\Gamma$ via \texttt{GppDigamma.digamma} --- see the QG-Blackbody chapter ---
  but the specific Gauss integral representation of $\psi$ at general $r$ this lemma would
  need is a further step not yet established, distinct from the special values and functional
  equation already proved.) $A_\infty$ itself is not assembled in this file.
\end{lemma}

\section{Honest Boundary}

Not claimed in this chapter: Bernstein's theorem / the Hausdorff--Widder characterization of
completely monotone functions as Laplace transforms of positive measures (needed for the
heat-trace criterion itself, still absent from Mathlib at the pin), the digamma integral
representation at general $r$ (needed to assemble the full Archimedean Laplace transform
$A_\infty$ --- see the QG-Blackbody chapter for what is now available), and the heat-trace
criterion RH $\iff \mathcal{K}$ completely monotone, as a single theorem. Nothing in this
chapter proves or assumes RH.

\chapter{The Digamma Function}

Earlier chapters (Thread HT, QG-Blackbody's kinematic-block gap) recorded ``Mathlib has no
digamma/polygamma function'' as a blocking gap, from a \texttt{grep} for the \emph{name}
\texttt{digamma}/\texttt{polygamma} in \texttt{.lake/packages/mathlib/} (zero hits at pinned
\texttt{v4.19.0}). That was the wrong question: Mathlib's
\texttt{NumberTheory.Harmonic.GammaDeriv} already computes \texttt{deriv Real.Gamma} in
closed form at $1$ and $1/2$ (Bohr--Mollerup convexity; Legendre's duplication formula) and
at every positive integer. \texttt{GppDigamma.digamma := deriv Gamma / Gamma} makes the
values below immediate corollaries.

\begin{theorem}[Digamma at 1]
  \label{thm:digamma-one}
  \lean{GppDigamma.digamma_one}
  \leanok
  $\psi(1) = -\gamma$, the Euler--Mascheroni constant.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Digamma at 1/2]
  \label{thm:digamma-one-half}
  \lean{GppDigamma.digamma_one_half}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:digamma-one}
  $\psi(1/2) = -\gamma - 2\log 2$, via Legendre's duplication formula (already differentiated
  in Mathlib).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Digamma at positive integers]
  \label{thm:digamma-nat}
  \lean{GppDigamma.digamma_nat_add_one}
  \leanok
  $\psi(n+1) = -\gamma + H_n$ (the $n$-th harmonic number), for every $n\in\mathbb{N}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Digamma functional equation]
  \label{thm:digamma-add-one}
  \lean{GppDigamma.digamma_add_one}
  \leanok
  $\psi(x+1) = \psi(x) + 1/x$, for $x$ avoiding the poles of $\Gamma$.
\end{theorem}

\textbf{Honest boundary.} This is the \emph{real}-argument digamma function only.
\texttt{kinematic\_block\_v1.tex}'s First Moment Theorem needs $\mathrm{Re}\,
\psi(1/2+i\lambda/2)$, the \emph{complex} digamma along a vertical line --- a further,
separate extension, not attempted here. The Gauss integral representation of $\psi$ at
general real $r$ (needed by Thread HT's Archimedean Laplace transform) is likewise not yet
established.

\section{The closing third of the First Moment Theorem}

\texttt{principal\_series\_blocks\_v2.tex}'s Theorem~6.1 (\texttt{thm:moment}) asserts
\[
  \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} P(\lambda)\,
  \mathrm{Re}\,\psi\!\left(\tfrac12+\tfrac{i\lambda}{2}\right)\dd\lambda
  = \frac18 + \frac14\,\psi\!\left(\tfrac12\right),
  \qquad P(\lambda)=\frac{\pi\lambda}{\sinh(\pi\lambda)} .
\]
Its proof has three inputs. The results below are the third --- the one that produces the
$1/8$ --- and they are unconditional. \textbf{Theorem~6.1 itself is not proved and is not
stated in Lean.}

\begin{theorem}[Gauss kernel as a hyperbolic one]
  \label{thm:fm-exp-sinh}
  \lean{GppFirstMoment.exp_div_one_sub_exp_eq}
  \leanok
  $e^{-t/2}/(1-e^{-t}) = 1/(2\sinh(t/2))$ for every $t>0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The hyperbolic collapse, with its Jacobian]
  \label{thm:fm-collapse}
  \lean{GppFirstMoment.collapse}
  \leanok
  $\dfrac{1}{2\sinh 2u}\cdot\dfrac{1-\operatorname{sech}^2u}{4}
   = \dfrac{1}{16}\,\tanh u\,\operatorname{sech}^2u$ for every real $u$.
  The paper performs this in one line as ``the integrand collapses to
  $\tanh u\operatorname{sech}^2u$''; that line silently carries the substitution $u=t/4$,
  and hence the constant $1/16$ made explicit here.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The elementary integral]
  \label{thm:fm-tanh-sech}
  \lean{GppFirstMoment.integral_tanh_mul_sech_sq}
  \leanok
  $\int_0^\infty \tanh u\,\operatorname{sech}^2u\,\dd u = 1/2$, by the fundamental theorem
  of calculus with antiderivative $-1/(2\cosh^2 u)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The remainder integral is $1/8$]
  \label{thm:fm-remainder}
  \lean{GppFirstMoment.remainder_integral_eq_one_eighth}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:fm-collapse, thm:fm-tanh-sech}
  \[
    \int_0^\infty \frac{1}{2\sinh(t/2)}\cdot
    \frac{1-\operatorname{sech}^2(t/4)}{4}\,\dd t \;=\; \frac18 .
  \]
  This is where Theorem~6.1's $1/8$ comes from.
\end{theorem}

\textbf{Honest boundary.} Two inputs remain, and neither is stated in Lean --- not as an
axiom, not as a \texttt{sorry}, not as a \texttt{True}-valued stub. First, the Fourier pair
$\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}} P(\lambda)\cos(\lambda y)\dd\lambda
= 1/(4\cosh^2(y/2))$, used at $y=0$ to give $\frac{1}{2\pi}\int P=\frac14$ and at $y=t/2$ to
produce the integrand above; Mathlib has the Gaussian Fourier transform but nothing for
$\operatorname{sech}^2$. Second, Gauss's integral representation
$\psi(s)=-\gamma+\int_0^\infty (e^{-t}-e^{-st})/(1-e^{-t})\dd t$, together with the complex
digamma along $\mathrm{Re}\,s=1/2$. The exchange of integration order between them is
Tonelli on a positive integrand and is routine once both exist.

\chapter{Local-Field Shadow Kernels}

From Daniel Toupin's ``Local-field shadow kernels, celestial unitarity, and the adelic
principal series'' (2026) --- a new research front investigating whether celestial Cutkosky
unitarity, 1D Mellin/shadow harmonic analysis, and adelic PGL(2) harmonic analysis share a
common rank-one local-to-global structure. \textbf{Not an RH proof; not evidence toward RH.}
Full honest boundary, including the paper's own open research problems, in
\texttt{discovery/local\_field\_shadow/local\_shadow\_kernel\_notes.md}.

\begin{theorem}[Archimedean Shadow Reflection]
  \label{thm:arch-kernel-reflection}
  \lean{GppLocalShadow.archKernel_reflection}
  \leanok
  For the Archimedean shadow kernel $K_{\infty,d}(a) := \Gamma(a)\Gamma(d-a)/\Gamma(d)$:
  $K_{\infty,d}(a) = K_{\infty,d}(d-a)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Principal-Series Positivity]
  \label{thm:arch-kernel-positivity}
  \lean{GppLocalShadow.archKernel_principal_series}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:arch-kernel-reflection}
  On the principal series $a=d/2+it$: $K_{\infty,d}(a) = |\Gamma(d/2+it)|^2/\Gamma(d)$,
  positive whenever $d>0$. Shadow reflection becomes Hermitian conjugation exactly on this
  line, via $d-a=\overline{a}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Diagonal Conformal Lift]
  \label{thm:diagonal-lift}
  \lean{GppDiagonalLift.delta_D}
  \leanok
  The diagonal lift $D(s):=(s,s)$ of a scalar 1D weight has $\Delta(D(s))=2s$ and spin
  $J(D(s))=0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Shadow Compatibility]
  \label{thm:shadow-compatibility}
  \lean{GppDiagonalLift.D_one_sub_eq_shadow2_D}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:diagonal-lift}
  $D(1-s) = \mathrm{Shadow}_2(D(s))$: the 1D shadow $s\mapsto1-s$ and the 2D celestial shadow
  $(h,\bar h)\mapsto(1-h,1-\bar h)$ intertwine exactly under the diagonal lift.
\end{theorem}

\section{The \texorpdfstring{$d=2$}{d=2} Cut vs. the Spherical Weyl Coefficient}

The naive conjecture that a single local factor $a_\infty(s)$ gives \emph{both} the physical
kernel $C_\infty=a_\infty(s)a_\infty(1-s)$ \emph{and} the normalized Weyl/Gindikin--Karpelevich
intertwiner $M_\infty=a_\infty(1-s)/a_\infty(s)$ is \textbf{false} --- confirmed numerically
